<issue_description>
Cycle flow error with apply_map
Hi:

## Description
Using `apply_map`with multiple variables returns cyclical graph.  The `apply_map` function adds a "**random**" dependency from a downstream task to an upstream task.

For more context see [slack thread](https://prefect-community.slack.com/archives/CL09KU1K7/p1607732581070700)

## Reproduction
```
from prefect import Flow, task, case, apply_map, Parameter, unmapped
from prefect.tasks.control_flow import merge

@task
def generate_input1():
    return{
        "num":1,
        "text": "hello"
    }
@task
def generate_input2():
    return {
        "num":2,
        "text": "bye"
    }


@task
def inc(input1, squared):
    return (input1["num"] + 1)**squared

@task
def negate(input1, input2):
    return input1.extend(input2)

@task
def is_even(input1):
    return input1["num"] % 2 == 0

def inc_or_negate(input1, input2, squared):
    
    cond = is_even(input1)

    # If x is even, increment it
    with case(cond, True):
        res1 = inc(input1, unmapped(squared))

    # If x is odd, negate it
    with case(cond, False):
        res2 = negate(input1, input2)
    
    return merge(res1, res2)

with Flow("apply-map example") as flow:
    
    squared = Parameter("squared", default=2)

    input1 = generate_input1.map()
    input2 = generate_input2.map()
    
    result = apply_map(inc_or_negate, input1, input2, squared)


flow.visualize()

state = flow.run()

flow.visualize(flow_state=state)
```

![image](https://user-images.githubusercontent.com/16763201/103023264-4046a980-4513-11eb-856b-3299e8a4093d.png)



## Environment
version `0.14.00`

Optionally run `prefect diagnostics` from the command line and paste the information here. -->

</issue_description>

### 1.1 Problem Analysis

The issue occurs when using Prefect's `apply_map` function with multiple variables. The function is supposed to apply a function to mapped inputs, but it's creating incorrect dependencies that result in cyclic graphs.

### 1.2 Technical Details Highlighted

- **Method**: `apply_map`
- **Functions**: `generate_input1`, `generate_input2`, `inc`, `negate`, `is_even`, `inc_or_negate`
- **Control flow**: `case`, `merge` 
- **Parameters**: `squared` parameter with `unmapped`
- **Error**: Cyclical graph dependencies
- **Version**: 0.14.00

### 1.3 Clear Problem Statement

When `apply_map` is used with multiple mapped inputs and a function containing control flow logic (like `case` statements and `merge`), it incorrectly adds dependencies between tasks that create cycles in the execution graph. This prevents the flow from running properly.

### 1.4 Reproduction Steps

1. Create tasks that generate input data (`generate_input1`, `generate_input2`)
2. Map these tasks to create multiple instances
3. Define a function (`inc_or_negate`) that uses control flow (`case` and `merge`)
4. Use `apply_map` to apply this function to the mapped inputs plus additional parameters
5. Visualize or run the flow - cycles appear in the graph

### 1.5 Best Practices for Testing/Fixing

- Understand the internal workings of `apply_map`
- Preserve intended semantics while removing spurious dependencies
- Test with various combinations of mapped/unmapped inputs
- Ensure control flow constructs still work correctly
- Don't break existing functionality

    31	
    32	## Hello, world! 👋
    33	
    34	We've rebuilt data engineering for the data science era.
    35	
    36	Prefect is a new workflow management system, designed for modern infrastructure and powered by the open-source Prefect Core workflow engine. Users organize `Tasks` into `Flows`, and Prefect takes care of the rest.
    37	
    38	Read the [docs](https://docs.prefect.io); get the [code](#installation); ask us [anything](https://prefect-slackin.herokuapp.com/)!
    39	
    40	### Welcome to Workflows
    41	
    42	Prefect's Pythonic API should feel familiar for newcomers. Mark functions as tasks and call them on each other to build up a flow.
    43	
    44	```python
    45	from prefect import task, Flow, Parameter
    46	
    47	
    48	@task(log_stdout=True)
    49	def say_hello(name):
    50	    print("Hello, {}!".format(name))
    51	
    52	
    53	with Flow("My First Flow") as flow:
    54	    name = Parameter('name')
    55	    say_hello(name)
    56	
    57	
    58	flow.run(name='world') # "Hello, world!"
    59	flow.run(name='Marvin') # "Hello, Marvin!"
    60	```
    61	
    62	For more detail, please see the [Core docs](https://docs.prefect.io/core/)
    63	
    64	### UI and Server
    65	
    66	<p align="center" style="margin-bottom:40px;">
    67	<img src="https://raw.githubusercontent.com/PrefectHQ/prefect/master/docs/.vuepress/public/orchestration/ui/dashboard-overview2.png"  height=440 style="max-height: 440px;">
    68	</p>
    69	
    70	In addition to the [Prefect Cloud](https://www.prefect.io/cloud) platform, Prefect includes an open-source backend for orchestrating and managing flows, consisting primarily of [Prefect Server](https://github.com/prefecthq/server) and [Prefect UI](https://github.com/prefecthq/ui). This local server stores flow metadata in a Postgres database and exposes a GraphQL API.
    71	
    72	Before running the server for the first time, run `prefect backend server` to configure Prefect for local orchestration. Please note the server requires [Docker](https://www.docker.com/) and [Docker Compose](https://docs.docker.com/compose/install/) to be running.
    73	
    74	To start the server, UI, and all required infrastructure, run:
    75	
    76	```
    77	prefect server start
    78	```
    79	
    80	Once all components are running, you can view the UI by visiting [http://localhost:8080](http://localhost:8080).
    81	
    82	Please note that executing flows from the server requires at least one Prefect Agent to be running: `prefect agent local start`.
    83	
    84	Finally, to register any flow with the server, call `flow.register()`. For more detail, please see the [orchestration docs](https://docs.prefect.io/orchestration/).
    85	
    86	## "...Prefect?"
    87	
    88	From the Latin _praefectus_, meaning "one who is in charge", a prefect is an official who oversees a domain and makes sure that the rules are followed. Similarly, Prefect is responsible for making sure that workflows execute properly.
    89	
    90	It also happens to be the name of a roving researcher for that wholly remarkable book, _The Hitchhiker's Guide to the Galaxy_.
    91	
    92	## Integrations
    93	
    94	Thanks to Prefect's growing task library and deep ecosystem integrations, building data applications is easier than ever.
    95	
    96	Something missing? Open a [feature request](https://github.com/PrefectHQ/prefect/issues/new/choose) or [contribute a PR](https://docs.prefect.io/core/development/overview.html)! Prefect was designed to make adding new functionality extremely easy, whether you build on top of the open-source package or maintain an internal task library for your team.

   117	## Resources
   118	
   119	Prefect provides a variety of resources to help guide you to a successful outcome.
   120	
   121	We are committed to ensuring a positive environment, and all interactions are governed by our [Code of Conduct](https://docs.prefect.io/core/code_of_conduct.html).
   122	
   123	### Documentation
   124	
   125	Prefect's documentation -- including concepts, tutorials, and a full API reference -- is always available at [docs.prefect.io](https://docs.prefect.io).
   126	
   127	Instructions for contributing to documentation can be found in the [development guide](https://docs.prefect.io/core/development/documentation.html).
   128	
   129	### Slack Community
   130	
   131	Join our [Slack](https://prefect-slackin.herokuapp.com/) to chat about Prefect, ask questions, and share tips.
   132	
   133	### Blog
   134	
   135	Visit the [Prefect Blog](https://medium.com/the-prefect-blog) for updates and insights from the Prefect team.
   136	
   137	### Support
   138	
   139	Prefect offers a variety of community and premium [support options](https://www.prefect.io/support) for users of both Prefect Core and Prefect Cloud.
   140	
   141	### Contributing
   142	
   143	Read about Prefect's [community](https://docs.prefect.io/core/community.html) or dive in to the [development guides](https://docs.prefect.io/core/development/overview.html) for information about contributions, documentation, code style, and testing.
   144	
   145	## Installation
   146	
   147	### Requirements
   148	
   149	Prefect requires Python 3.6+. If you're new to Python, we recommend installing the [Anaconda distribution](https://www.anaconda.com/distribution/).
   150	
   151	### Latest Release
   152	
   153	To install Prefect, run:
   154	
   155	```bash
   156	pip install prefect
   157	```
   158	
   159	or, if you prefer to use `conda`:
   160	
   161	```bash
   162	conda install -c conda-forge prefect
   163	```
   164	
   165	or `pipenv`:
   166	
   167	```bash
   168	pipenv install --pre prefect
   169	```
   170	
   171	### Bleeding Edge
   172	
   173	For development or just to try out the latest features, you may want to install Prefect directly from source.
   174	
   175	Please note that the master branch of Prefect is not guaranteed to be compatible with Prefect Cloud or the local server.
   176	
   177	```bash
   178	git clone https://github.com/PrefectHQ/prefect.git

     1	import itertools
     2	import warnings
     3	from collections.abc import Sequence
     4	from contextlib import contextmanager
     5	from datetime import timedelta
     6	from functools import wraps
     7	from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any, Callable, Iterator, Optional, overload, Union
     8	
     9	import pendulum
    10	
    11	import prefect
    12	
    13	__all__ = [
    14	    "tags",
    15	    "as_task",
    16	    "pause_task",
    17	    "task",
    18	    "apply_map",
    19	    "defaults_from_attrs",
    20	]
    21	
    22	if TYPE_CHECKING:
    23	    import prefect.tasks.core.constants
    24	    import prefect.tasks.core.collections
    25	    import prefect.tasks.core.function
    26	    from prefect import Flow
    27	
    28	
    29	def apply_map(func: Callable, *args: Any, flow: "Flow" = None, **kwargs: Any) -> Any:
    30	    """
    31	    Map a function that adds tasks to a flow elementwise across one or more
    32	    tasks.  Arguments that should _not_ be mapped over should be wrapped with
    33	    `prefect.unmapped`.
    34	
    35	    This can be useful when wanting to create complicated mapped pipelines
    36	    (e.g. ones using control flow components like `case`).
    37	
    38	    Args:
    39	        - func (Callable): a function that adds tasks to a flow
    40	        - *args: task arguments to map over
    41	        - flow (Flow, optional): The flow to use, defaults to the current flow
    42	            in context if no flow is specified. If specified, `func` must accept
    43	            a `flow` keyword argument.
    44	        - **kwargs: keyword task arguments to map over
    45	
    46	    Returns:
    47	        - Any: the output of `func`, if any
    48	
    49	    Example:
    50	
    51	    ```python
    52	    from prefect import task, case, apply_map
    53	    from prefect.tasks.control_flow import merge
    54	
    55	    @task
    56	    def inc(x):
    57	        return x + 1
    58	
    59	    @task
    60	    def is_even(x):
    61	        return x % 2 == 0
    62	
    63	    def inc_if_even(x):
    64	        with case(is_even(x), True):
    65	            x2 = inc(x)
    66	        return merge(x, x2)
    67	
    68	    with Flow("example") as flow:
    69	        apply_map(inc_if_even, range(10))
    70	    ```
    71	    """
    72	    from prefect.tasks.core.constants import Constant
    73	
    74	    no_flow_provided = flow is None
    75	    if no_flow_provided:
    76	        flow = prefect.context.get("flow", None)
    77	        if flow is None:
    78	            raise ValueError("Couldn't infer a flow in the current context")
    79	    assert isinstance(flow, prefect.Flow)  # appease mypy
    80	
    81	    # Check if args/kwargs are valid first
    82	    for x in itertools.chain(args, kwargs.values()):
    83	        if not isinstance(
    84	            x, (prefect.Task, prefect.utilities.edges.EdgeAnnotation, Sequence)
    85	        ):
    86	            raise TypeError(
    87	                f"Cannot map over non-sequence object of type `{type(x).__name__}`"
    88	            )
    89	
    90	    flow2 = prefect.Flow("temporary flow")
    91	    # A mapping of all the input args -> (is_mapped, is_constant)
    92	    arg_info = {}
    93	    # A mapping of the ids of all constants -> the Constant task.
    94	    # Used to convert constants to constant tasks if needed
    95	    id_to_const = {}
    96	
    97	    # Preprocess inputs to `apply_map`:
    98	    # - Extract information about each argument (is unmapped, is constant, ...)
    99	    # - Convert all arguments to instances of `Task`
   100	    # - Add all non-constant arguments to the flow. Constant arguments are
   101	    #   added later as needed.
   102	    def preprocess(a: Any) -> "prefect.Task":
   103	        a2 = as_task(a, flow=flow2)
   104	        is_mapped = not isinstance(a, prefect.utilities.edges.unmapped)
   105	        is_constant = isinstance(a2, Constant)
   106	        arg_info[a2] = (is_mapped, is_constant)
   107	        if not is_constant:
   108	            flow.add_task(a2)  # type: ignore
   109	        if is_mapped and is_constant:
   110	            id_to_const[id(a2.value)] = a2  # type: ignore
   111	        return a2
   112	
   113	    args2 = [preprocess(a) for a in args]
   114	    kwargs2 = {k: preprocess(v) for k, v in kwargs.items()}
   115	
   116	    # Construct a temporary flow for the subgraph
   117	    # We set case=None & resource=None to ignore any external case/resource
   118	    # blocks while constructing the temporary flow.
   119	    with prefect.context(mapped=True, case=None, resource=None):
   120	        with flow2:
   121	            if no_flow_provided:
   122	                res = func(*args2, **kwargs2)
   123	            else:
   124	                res = func(*args2, flow=flow2, **kwargs2)
   125	
   126	    # Copy over all tasks in the subgraph
   127	    for task in flow2.tasks:
   128	        flow.add_task(task)
   129	
   130	    # Copy over all edges, updating any non-explicitly-unmapped edges to mapped
   131	    for edge in flow2.edges:
   132	        flow.add_edge(
   133	            upstream_task=edge.upstream_task,
   134	            downstream_task=edge.downstream_task,
   135	            key=edge.key,
   136	            mapped=arg_info.get(edge.upstream_task, (True,))[0],
   137	        )
   138	
   139	    # Copy over all constants, updating any constants that should be mapped
   140	    # to be tasks rather than stored in the constants mapping
   141	    for task, constants in flow2.constants.items():
   142	        for key, c in constants.items():
   143	            if id(c) in id_to_const:
   144	                c_task = id_to_const[id(c)]
   145	                flow.add_task(c_task)
   146	                flow.add_edge(
   147	                    upstream_task=c_task, downstream_task=task, key=key, mapped=True
   148	                )
   149	            else:
   150	                flow.constants[task][key] = c
   151	
   152	    # Any task created inside `apply_map` must have a transitive dependency to
   153	    # all of the inputs to apply_map, except for unmapped constants.  This
   154	    # ensures three things:
   155	    #
   156	    # - All mapped arguments must have the same length. supporting disparate
   157	    # lengths leads to odd semantics.
   158	    #
   159	    # - Tasks created by `apply_map` conceptually share an upstream dependency
   160	    # tree. This matches the causality you'd expect if you were running as
   161	    # normal eager python code - the stuff inside the `apply_map` only runs if
   162	    # the inputs are completed, not just the inputs that certain subcomponents
   163	    # depend on.
   164	    #
   165	    # - Tasks with no external dependencies are treated the same as tasks with
   166	    # external deps (we need to add upstream_tasks to tasks created in `func`
   167	    # with no external deps to get them to run as proper map tasks).  We add
   168	    # upstream tasks uniformly for all tasks, not just ones without external
   169	    # deps - the uniform behavior makes this easier to reason about.
   170	    #
   171	    # Here we do a final pass adding missing upstream deps on mapped arguments
   172	    # to all newly created tasks in the apply_map.
   173	    new_tasks = flow2.tasks.difference(arg_info)
   174	    for task in new_tasks:
   175	        upstream_tasks = flow.upstream_tasks(task)
   176	        is_root_in_subgraph = not upstream_tasks.intersection(new_tasks)
   177	        if is_root_in_subgraph:
   178	            for arg_task, (is_mapped, is_constant) in arg_info.items():
   179	                # Add all args except unmapped constants as direct
   180	                # upstream tasks if they're not already upstream tasks
   181	                if arg_task not in upstream_tasks and (is_mapped or not is_constant):
   182	                    flow.add_edge(
   183	                        upstream_task=arg_task, downstream_task=task, mapped=is_mapped
   184	                    )
   185	    return res
   186	
   187	
   188	# DEPRECATED backward-compatible import
   189	from prefect.utilities.edges import unmapped as _unmapped
   190	
   191	
   192	def unmapped(*args, **kwargs):  # type: ignore
   193	    warnings.warn(
   194	        "`unmapped` has moved, please import as `prefect.utilities.edges.unmapped`",
   195	        stacklevel=2,
   196	    )
   197	    return _unmapped(*args, **kwargs)
   198	
   199	
   200	@contextmanager
   201	def tags(*tags: str) -> Iterator[None]:
   202	    """
   203	    Context manager for setting task tags.
   204	
   205	    Args:
   206	        - *tags ([str]): a list of tags to apply to the tasks created within
   207	            the context manager
   208	
   209	    Example:
   210	    ```python
   211	    @task
   212	    def add(x, y):
   213	        return x + y
   214	
   215	    with Flow("My Flow") as f:
   216	        with tags("math", "function"):
   217	            result = add(1, 5)
   218	
   219	    print(result.tags) # {"function", "math"}
   220	    ```
   221	    """
   222	    tags_set = set(tags)
   223	    tags_set.update(prefect.context.get("tags", set()))
   224	    with prefect.context(tags=tags_set):
   225	        yield
   226	
   227	
   228	def as_task(x: Any, flow: "Optional[Flow]" = None) -> "prefect.Task":
   229	    """
   230	    Wraps a function, collection, or constant with the appropriate Task type. If a constant
   231	    or collection of constants is passed, a `Constant` task is returned.
   232	
   233	    Args:
   234	        - x (object): any Python object to convert to a prefect Task
   235	        - flow (Flow, optional): Flow to which the prefect Task will be bound
   236	
   237	    Returns:
   238	        - a prefect Task representing the passed object
   239	    """
   240	    from prefect.tasks.core.constants import Constant
   241	
   242	    def is_constant(x: Any) -> bool:
   243	        """
   244	        Helper function for determining if nested collections are constants without calling
   245	        `bind()`, which would create new tasks on the active graph.
   246	        """
   247	        if isinstance(x, (prefect.core.Task, prefect.utilities.edges.EdgeAnnotation)):
   248	            return False
   249	        elif isinstance(x, (list, tuple, set)):
   250	            return all(is_constant(xi) for xi in x)
   251	        elif isinstance(x, dict):
   252	            return all(is_constant(xi) for xi in x.values())
   253	        return True
   254	
   255	    # task objects
   256	    if isinstance(x, prefect.core.Task):  # type: ignore
   257	        return x
   258	    elif isinstance(x, prefect.utilities.edges.EdgeAnnotation):
   259	        return as_task(x.value, flow=flow)
   260	
   261	    # handle constants, including collections of constants
   262	    elif is_constant(x):
   263	        return_task = Constant(x)  # type: prefect.core.Task
   264	
   265	    # collections
   266	    elif isinstance(x, list):
   267	        return_task = prefect.tasks.core.collections.List().bind(*x, flow=flow)
   268	    elif isinstance(x, tuple):
   269	        return_task = prefect.tasks.core.collections.Tuple().bind(*x, flow=flow)
   270	    elif isinstance(x, set):
   271	        return_task = prefect.tasks.core.collections.Set().bind(*x, flow=flow)
   272	    elif isinstance(x, dict):
   273	        keys, values = [], []
   274	        for k, v in x.items():
   275	            keys.append(k)
   276	            values.append(v)
   277	        return_task = prefect.tasks.core.collections.Dict().bind(
   278	            keys=keys, values=values, flow=flow
   279	        )
   280	
   281	    else:
   282	        return x
   283	
   284	    return_task.auto_generated = True  # type: ignore
   285	    return return_task
   286	
   287	
   288	def pause_task(message: str = None, duration: timedelta = None) -> None:
   289	    """
   290	    Utility function for pausing a task during execution to wait for manual intervention.
   291	    Note that the _entire task_ will be rerun if the user decides to run this task again!
   292	    The only difference is that this utility will _not_ raise a `PAUSE` signal.
   293	    To bypass a `PAUSE` signal being raised, put the task into a Resume state.
   294	
   295	    Args:
   296	        - message (str): an optional message for the Pause state.
   297	        - duration (timedelta): an optional pause duration; otherwise infinite (well, 10 years)
   298	
   299	    Example:
   300	        ```python
   301	        from prefect import Flow
   302	        from prefect.utilities.tasks import task, pause_task
   303	
   304	        @task
   305	        def add(x, y):
   306	            z = y - x  ## this code will be rerun after resuming from the pause!
   307	            if z == 0: ## this code will be rerun after resuming from the pause!
   308	                pause_task()
   309	            return x + y
   310	
   311	        with Flow("My Flow") as f:
   312	            res = add(4, 4)
   313	
   314	        state = f.run()
   315	        state.result[res] # a Paused state
   316	
   317	        state = f.run(task_states={res: Resume()})
   318	        state.result[res] # a Success state
   319	        ```
   320	    """
   321	    if duration is not None:
   322	        start_time = pendulum.now("utc") + duration
   323	    else:
   324	        start_time = None
   325	    if prefect.context.get("resume", False) is False:
   326	        raise prefect.engine.signals.PAUSE(  # type: ignore
   327	            message=message or "Pause signal raised during task execution.",
   328	            start_time=start_time,
   329	        )
   330	
   331	
   332	# To support mypy type checking with optional arguments to `task`, we need to
   333	# make use of `typing.overload`
   334	@overload
   335	def task(__fn: Callable) -> "prefect.tasks.core.function.FunctionTask":
   336	    pass
   337	
   338	
   339	@overload
   340	def task(
   341	    **task_init_kwargs: Any,
   342	) -> Callable[[Callable], "prefect.tasks.core.function.FunctionTask"]:
   343	    pass
   344	
   345	
   346	def task(
   347	    fn: Callable = None, **task_init_kwargs: Any
   348	) -> Union[
   349	    "prefect.tasks.core.function.FunctionTask",
   350	    Callable[[Callable], "prefect.tasks.core.function.FunctionTask"],
   351	]:
   352	    """
   353	    A decorator for creating Tasks from functions.
   354	
   355	    Args:
   356	        - fn (Callable): the decorated function
   357	        - **task_init_kwargs (Any): keyword arguments that will be passed to the `Task`
   358	            constructor on initialization.
   359	
   360	    Returns:
   361	        - FunctionTask: A instance of a FunctionTask
   362	
   363	    Raises:
   364	        - ValueError: if the provided function violates signature requirements
   365	            for Task run methods
   366	
   367	    Usage:
   368	
   369	    ```
   370	    @task(name='hello', retries=3)
   371	    def hello(name):
   372	        print('hello, {}'.format(name))
   373	
   374	    with Flow("My Flow") as flow:
   375	        t1 = hello('foo')
   376	        t2 = hello('bar')
   377	
   378	    ```
   379	
   380	    The decorator is best suited to Prefect's functional API, but can also be used
   381	    with the imperative API.
   382	
   383	    ```
   384	    @task
   385	    def fn_without_args():
   386	        return 1
   387	
   388	    @task
   389	    def fn_with_args(x):
   390	        return x
   391	
   392	    # both tasks work inside a functional flow context
   393	    with Flow("My Flow"):
   394	        fn_without_args()
   395	        fn_with_args(1)
   396	    ```
   397	    """
   398	    if fn is None:
   399	        return lambda fn: prefect.tasks.core.function.FunctionTask(
   400	            fn=fn,
   401	            **task_init_kwargs,
   402	        )
   403	    return prefect.tasks.core.function.FunctionTask(fn=fn, **task_init_kwargs)
   404	
   405	
   406	def defaults_from_attrs(*attr_args: str) -> Callable:
   407	    """
   408	    Helper decorator for dealing with Task classes with attributes that serve
   409	    as defaults for `Task.run`.  Specifically, this decorator allows the author
   410	    of a Task to identify certain keyword arguments to the run method which
   411	    will fall back to `self.ATTR_NAME` if not explicitly provided to
   412	    `self.run`.  This pattern allows users to create a Task "template", whose
   413	    default settings can be created at initialization but overrided in
   414	    individual instances when the Task is called.
   415	
   416	    Args:
   417	        - *attr_args (str): a splatted list of strings specifying which
   418	            kwargs should fallback to attributes, if not provided at runtime. Note that
   419	            the strings provided here must match keyword arguments in the `run` call signature,
   420	            as well as the names of attributes of this Task.
   421	
   422	    Returns:
   423	        - Callable: the decorated / altered `Task.run` method
   424	
   425	    Example:
   426	    ```python
   427	    class MyTask(Task):
   428	        def __init__(self, a=None, b=None):
   429	            self.a = a
   430	            self.b = b
   431	
   432	        @defaults_from_attrs('a', 'b')
   433	        def run(self, a=None, b=None):
   434	            return a, b
   435	
   436	    task = MyTask(a=1, b=2)
   437	
   438	    task.run() # (1, 2)
   439	    task.run(a=99) # (99, 2)
   440	    task.run(a=None, b=None) # (None, None)
   441	    ```
   442	    """
   443	
   444	    def wrapper(run_method: Callable) -> Callable:
   445	        @wraps(run_method)
   446	        def method(self: Any, *args: Any, **kwargs: Any) -> Any:
   447	            for attr in attr_args:
   448	                kwargs.setdefault(attr, getattr(self, attr))
   449	            return run_method(self, *args, **kwargs)
   450	
   451	        return method
   452	
   453	    return wrapper

   257	    def test_tasks_have_all_non_unmapped_constant_args_as_transitive_upstream_deps(
   258	        self,
   259	    ):
   260	        def func(a, b, c, d):
   261	            m = inc.copy(name="m").bind(1)
   262	            n = inc.copy(name="n").bind(a)
   263	            o = inc.copy(name="o").bind(b)
   264	            p = add.copy(name="p").bind(n, o)
   265	            q = add.copy(name="q").bind(c, d)
   266	            r = add.copy(name="r").bind(q, m)
   267	            return m, n, o, p, q, r

   270	            a = ranged.copy(name="a").bind(3)
   271	            b = inc.copy(name="b").bind(1)
   272	            c = Constant(1, name="c")
   273	            d = Constant(range(3), name="d")

   275	                func, a, edges.unmapped(b), c=edges.unmapped(c), d=d
   276	            )
   277	
   278	        def edge_info(task):
   279	            """Returns a map of {upstream: (is_data_dep, is_mapped)}"""
   280	            return {
   281	                e.upstream_task: (e.key is not None, e.mapped)
   282	                for e in flow.edges_to(task)
   283	            }
   284

   286	        assert edge_info(n) == {a: (True, True), b: (False, False), d: (False, True)}

"""
Reproduction script for the cycle flow error with apply_map issue.
"""

from prefect import Flow, task, case, apply_map, Parameter, unmapped
from prefect.tasks.control_flow import merge

@task
def generate_input1():
    return{
        "num":1,
        "text": "hello"
    }

@task  
def generate_input2():
    return {
        "num":2,
        "text": "bye"
    }


@task
def inc(input1, squared):
    return (input1["num"] + 1)**squared

@task
def negate(input1, input2):
    # Fixed the method - extend doesn't work on dict, let's concatenate text
    return input1["text"] + input2["text"]

@task
def is_even(input1):
    return input1["num"] % 2 == 0

def inc_or_negate(input1, input2, squared):
    
    cond = is_even(input1)

    # If x is even, increment it
    with case(cond, True):
        res1 = inc(input1, unmapped(squared))

    # If x is odd, negate it
    with case(cond, False):
        res2 = negate(input1, input2)
    
    return merge(res1, res2)

def main():
    print("Creating flow...")
    with Flow("apply-map example") as flow:
        
        squared = Parameter("squared", default=2)

        input1 = generate_input1.map()
        input2 = generate_input2.map()
        
        result = apply_map(inc_or_negate, input1, input2, squared)

    print("Flow created. Attempting to visualize...")
    try:
        flow.visualize()
        print("Visualization successful!")
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"Visualization failed with error: {e}")
        
    print("\nAttempting to run flow...")
    try:
        state = flow.run()
        print(f"Flow run successful! State: {state}")
        if state.is_successful():
            print(f"Result: {state.result[result].result}")
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"Flow run failed with error: {e}")
        import traceback
        traceback.print_exc()

from prefect import Flow, task, case, apply_map, Parameter, unmapped
from prefect.tasks.control_flow import merge

@task
def generate_input1():

        "num":1,
        "text": "hello"
    }

@task  
def generate_input2():
    return {
        "num":2,
        "text": "bye"
    }


@task
def inc(input1, squared):
    return (input1["num"] + 1)**squared

@task
def negate(input1, input2):
    # Fixed the method - extend doesn't work on dict, let's concatenate text
    return input1["text"] + input2["text"]

@task
def is_even(input1):
    return input1["num"] % 2 == 0

def inc_or_negate(input1, input2, squared):
    
    cond = is_even(input1)

    # If x is even, increment it
    with case(cond, True):
        res1 = inc(input1, unmapped(squared))

    # If x is odd, negate it
    with case(cond, False):
        res2 = negate(input1, input2)
    
    return merge(res1, res2)

def main():
    print("Creating flow...")
    with Flow("apply-map example") as flow:
        
        squared = Parameter("squared", default=2)

        input1 = generate_input1.map()
        input2 = generate_input2.map()
        
        result = apply_map(inc_or_negate, input1, input2, squared)

    print(f"Total tasks in flow: {len(flow.tasks)}")
    print(f"Total edges in flow: {len(flow.edges)}")
    
    # Print all tasks
    print("\nTasks:")
    for i, task in enumerate(flow.tasks):
        print(f"  {i}: {task.name} ({task})")
    
    # Print edges to identify cycles
    print("\nEdges:")
    for i, edge in enumerate(flow.edges):
        print(f"  {i}: {edge.upstream_task.name} -> {edge.downstream_task.name} (mapped={edge.mapped})")
    
    # Check for cycles manually
    print("\nChecking for cycles...")
    try:
        sorted_tasks = flow.sorted_tasks()
        print(f"Topological sort successful: {len(sorted_tasks)} tasks")
    except ValueError as e:
        print(f"Topological sort failed: {e}")
        
        # Let's try to identify the problematic edges
        print("\nAnalyzing potential cycles...")
        # Look for bidirectional edges or self-loops
        task_names = {task: task.name for task in flow.tasks}
        edges_dict = {}
        for edge in flow.edges:
            upstream = edge.upstream_task
            downstream = edge.downstream_task
            if upstream not in edges_dict:
                edges_dict[upstream] = []
            edges_dict[upstream].append(downstream)
            
        print("Upstream -> Downstream relationships:")
        for upstream, downstreams in edges_dict.items():
            print(f"  {upstream.name}: {[d.name for d in downstreams]}")

Wait, let me check this more carefully. The issue seems to be that `case(False)` has an edge to `generate_input2`, which shouldn't happen. Let me trace this better.<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Looking at the debug output, I can see the problem clearly now:

The cycle is:
1. `is_even` -> `case(False)` (edge 0)  
2. `case(False)` -> `generate_input2` (edge 8)
3. `generate_input2` -> `is_even` (edge 13)

This creates: `is_even` → `case(False)` → `generate_input2` → `is_even` (cycle!)

But this doesn't make sense logically. Why would `case(False)` be connected to `generate_input2`? 

Looking at the original code:
```python
def inc_or_negate(input1, input2, squared):
    cond = is_even(input1)
    with case(cond, True):
        res1 = inc(input1, unmapped(squared))
    with case(cond, False):
        res2 = negate(input1, input2)
    return merge(res1, res2)
```

So `case(cond, False)` should only be connected to `negate`, not to `generate_input2`. 

The issue is in the `apply_map` function. In lines 171-185, it's adding upstream dependencies to ALL new tasks that are roots in the subgraph. But this is too aggressive - it's connecting tasks that shouldn't be connected.

Looking at the logic:
1. It finds tasks that are "roots in the subgraph" (no upstream tasks within the new_tasks set)
2. For each such root task, it connects ALL mapped arguments as upstream tasks

But this is wrong! The root tasks in the subgraph might already have their proper upstream dependencies. Adding ALL mapped arguments as upstream creates spurious connections that lead to cycles.

The problem is specifically in this part of the code:
```python
# Any task created inside `apply_map` must have a transitive dependency to
# all of the inputs to apply_map, except for unmapped constants.
```

This assumption is incorrect. Tasks should only depend on the arguments they actually use, not on all arguments passed to apply_map.

Looking at the debug output:
- `case(False)` is a root in the subgraph (it has no upstream tasks within new_tasks)
- So it gets connected to ALL mapped arguments: `generate_input1`, `generate_input2`, `squared`
- But `case(False)` should only be triggered by `is_even`, not by `generate_input2`

The fix should be to make the upstream dependency addition more selective - only add dependencies that make sense, not blanket dependencies on all arguments.</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS: Understanding the Problem and Solution

### 5.1 What the problem is:
The `apply_map` function incorrectly adds upstream dependencies from all root tasks in the subgraph to ALL mapped arguments, regardless of whether those dependencies make logical sense. This creates artificial cycles in the task graph.

### 5.2 Where the problem is located:
In `/workspace/PrefectHQ__prefect__0.14/src/prefect/utilities/tasks.py`, lines 171-185, specifically in the logic that adds upstream dependencies to root tasks.

### 5.3 How the test reproduces the issue:
The reproduction script creates a flow with `apply_map` that uses control flow (`case` statements). The `apply_map` function incorrectly connects `case(False)` to `generate_input2`, creating a cycle: `is_even` → `case(False)` → `generate_input2` → `is_even`.

### 5.4 Best practices to take into account:
- Preserve existing functionality and don't break legitimate use cases
- Only add necessary dependencies, not blanket dependencies
- Maintain the intended semantics of control flow constructs
- Ensure the fix handles various combinations of mapped/unmapped arguments

### 5.5 How to fix the problem:
Instead of blindly adding all mapped arguments as upstream dependencies to root tasks, we should:
1. Only add dependencies for arguments that are actually used by tasks in the subgraph
2. Or more conservatively, avoid adding dependencies that would create cycles
3. Or even better, reconsider whether this blanket dependency addition is necessary at all

Let me look at the actual logic again:

```python
new_tasks = flow2.tasks.difference(arg_info)
for task in new_tasks:
    upstream_tasks = flow.upstream_tasks(task)
    is_root_in_subgraph = not upstream_tasks.intersection(new_tasks)
    if is_root_in_subgraph:
        for arg_task, (is_mapped, is_constant) in arg_info.items():
            # Add all args except unmapped constants as direct
            # upstream tasks if they're not already upstream tasks
            if arg_task not in upstream_tasks and (is_mapped or not is_constant):
                flow.add_edge(
                    upstream_task=arg_task, downstream_task=task, mapped=is_mapped
                )
```

The issue is that `case` tasks ARE root tasks in the subgraph (they have no upstream tasks from within the new subgraph), so they get all the arguments added as upstream dependencies.

from prefect import Flow, task, case, apply_map, Parameter, unmapped
from prefect.tasks.control_flow import merge

@task
def generate_input1():
    return{
        "num":1,
        "text": "hello"
    }

@task  
def generate_input2():
    return {
        "num":2,
        "text": "bye"
    }

@task
def is_even(input1):
    return input1["num"] % 2 == 0

def inc_or_negate(input1, input2, squared):
    cond = is_even(input1)
    with case(cond, True):
        pass
    with case(cond, False):
        pass
    return merge(1, 2)

def main():
    print("Creating flow...")
    with Flow("debug") as flow:
        squared = Parameter("squared", default=2)
        input1 = generate_input1.map()
        input2 = generate_input2.map()

    for task in flow.tasks:
        print(f"  {task.name}: {type(task)}")
        if isinstance(task, CaseTask):
            print(f"    -> This is a CaseTask")
        elif isinstance(task, Merge):
            print(f"    -> This is a Merge task")

    12	class case(object):
    13	    """A conditional block in a flow definition.
    14	
    15	    Used as a context-manager, `case` creates a block of tasks that are only
    16	    run if the result of `task` is equal to `value`.
    17	
    18	    Args:
    19	        - task (Task): The task to use in the comparison
    20	        - value (Any): A constant the result of `task` will be compared with
    21	
    22	    Example:
    23	
    24	    A `case` block is similar to Python's if-blocks. It delimits a block
    25	    of tasks that will only be run if the result of `task` is equal to
    26	    `value`:
    27	
    28	    ```python
    29	    # Standard python code
    30	    if task == value:
    31	        res = run_if_task_equals_value()
    32	        other_task(res)
    33	
    34	    # Equivalent prefect code
    35	    with case(task, value):
    36	        # Tasks created in this block are only run if the
    37	        # result of `task` is equal to `value`
    38	        res = run_if_task_equals_value()
    39	        other_task(run)
    40	    ```
    41	
    42	    The `value` argument can be any non-task object. Here we branch on a
    43	    string result:
    44	
    45	    ```python
    46	    with Flow("example") as flow:
    47	        cond = condition()
    48	
    49	        with case(cond, "a"):
    50	            run_if_cond_is_a()
    51	
    52	        with case(cond, "b"):
    53	            run_if_cond_is_b()
    54	    ```
    55	    """
    56	
    57	    def __init__(self, task: Task, value: Any):
    58	        if isinstance(value, Task):
    59	            raise TypeError("`value` cannot be a task")
    60	
    61	        self.task = task
    62	        self.value = value
    63	        self._tasks = set()
    64	        self._flow = None
    65	
    66	    def add_task(self, task: Task, flow: Flow) -> None:
    67	        """Add a new task under the case statement.
    68	
    69	        Args:
    70	            - task (Task): the task to add
    71	            - flow (Flow): the flow to use
    72	        """
    73	        if self._flow is None:
    74	            self._flow = flow
    75	        elif self._flow is not flow:
    76	            raise ValueError(
    77	                "Multiple flows cannot be used with the same case statement"
    78	            )
    79	        self._tasks.add(task)
    80	
    81	    def __enter__(self):
    82	        self.__prev_case = prefect.context.get("case")
    83	        prefect.context.update(case=self)
    84	
    85	    def __exit__(self, *args):
    86	        if self.__prev_case is None:
    87	            prefect.context.pop("case", None)
    88	        else:
    89	            prefect.context.update(case=self.__prev_case)
    90	
    91	        if self._tasks:
    92	            cond = CompareValue(self.value, name=f"case({self.value})").bind(
    93	                value=self.task, flow=self._flow
    94	            )
    95	
    96	            for child in self._tasks:
    97	                # If a task has no upstream tasks created in this case block,
    98	                # the case conditional should be set as an upstream task.
    99	                if not self._tasks.intersection(self._flow.upstream_tasks(child)):
   100	                    child.set_upstream(cond, flow=self._flow)

     4	from prefect import Task

     6	
     7	__all__ = ["switch", "ifelse", "merge"]

    10	class Merge(Task):
    11	    def __init__(self, **kwargs) -> None:
    12	        if kwargs.setdefault("skip_on_upstream_skip", False):
    13	            raise ValueError("Merge tasks must have `skip_on_upstream_skip=False`.")
    14	        kwargs.setdefault("trigger", prefect.triggers.not_all_skipped)
    15	        super().__init__(**kwargs)
    16	
    17	    def run(self, **task_results: Any) -> Any:
    18	        return next((v for k, v in sorted(task_results.items()) if v is not None), None)
    19	
    20	
    21	class CompareValue(Task):
    22	    """
    23	    This task stores a `value` at initialization and compares it to a `value` received at runtime.
    24	    If the values don't match, it raises a SKIP exception.
    25	
    26	    Args:
    27	        - value (Any): the value this task will attempt to match when it runs
    28	        - **kwargs: keyword arguments for the Task
    29	    """
    30	
    31	    def __init__(self, value: Any, **kwargs: Any):
    32	        self.value = value
    33	        kwargs.setdefault("name", 'CompareValue: "{}"'.format(value))
    34	        super().__init__(**kwargs)
    35	
    36	    def run(self, value: Any) -> None:
    37	        """
    38	        Raises a SKIP signal if the passed value does not match the task's match value;
    39	        succeeds silently otherwise.
    40	
    41	        Args:
    42	            - value (Any): the value that will be matched against the task's value.
    43	        """
    44	        if value != self.value:
    45	            raise signals.SKIP(
    46	                'Provided value "{}" did not match "{}"'.format(value, self.value)
    47	            )
    48	
    49	
    50	def switch(condition: Task, cases: Dict[Any, Task], mapped: bool = False) -> None:
    51	    """
    52	    Adds a SWITCH to a workflow.
    53	
    54	    The condition task is evaluated and the result is compared to the keys of the cases
    55	    dictionary. The task corresponding to the matching key is run; all other tasks are
    56	    skipped. Any tasks downstream of the skipped tasks are also skipped unless they set
    57	    `skip_on_upstream_skip=False`.
    58	
    59	    Example:
    60	    ```python
    61	    @task
    62	    def condition():
    63	        return "b"    # returning 'b' will take the b_branch
    64	
    65	    @task
    66	    def a_branch():
    67	        return "A Branch"
    68	
    69	    @task
    70	    def b_branch():
    71	        return "B Branch"
    72	
    73	    with Flow("switch-flow") as flow:
    74	        switch(condition, dict(a=a_branch, b=b_branch))
    75	    ```
    76	
    77	    Args:
    78	        - condition (Task): a task whose result forms the condition for the switch
    79	        - cases (Dict[Any, Task]): a dict representing the "case" statements of the switch.
    80	            The value of the `condition` task will be compared to the keys of this dict, and
    81	            the matching task will be executed.
    82	        - mapped (bool, optional): If true, the `switch` operation will be mapped over the
    83	            arguments instead of applied directly. Defaults to `False`.
    84	
    85	    Raises:
    86	        - PrefectWarning: if any of the tasks in "cases" have upstream dependencies,
    87	            then this task will warn that those upstream tasks may run whether or not the
    88	            switch condition matches their branch. The most common cause of this is passing a
    89	            list of tasks as one of the cases, which adds the `List` task to the switch
    90	            condition but leaves the tasks themselves upstream.
    91	    """
    92	
    93	    with prefect.tags("switch"):
    94	        for value, task in cases.items():
    95	            task = prefect.utilities.tasks.as_task(task)
    96	            match_condition = CompareValue(value=value).bind(
    97	                value=condition, mapped=mapped
    98	            )
    99	            task.set_dependencies(upstream_tasks=[match_condition], mapped=mapped)
   100	
   101	
   102	def ifelse(
   103	    condition: Task, true_task: Task, false_task: Task, mapped: bool = False
   104	) -> None:
   105	    """
   106	    Builds a conditional branch into a workflow.
   107	
   108	    If the condition evaluates True(ish), the true_task will run. If it
   109	    evaluates False(ish), the false_task will run. The task doesn't run is Skipped, as are
   110	    all downstream tasks that don't set `skip_on_upstream_skip=False`.
   111	
   112	    Args:
   113	        - condition (Task): a task whose boolean result forms the condition for the ifelse
   114	        - true_task (Task): a task that will be executed if the condition is True
   115	        - false_task (Task): a task that will be executed if the condition is False
   116	        - mapped (bool, optional): If true, the `ifelse` operation will be mapped over the
   117	            arguments instead of applied directly. Defaults to `False`.
   118	    """
   119	
   120	    @prefect.task
   121	    def as_bool(x):
   122	        return bool(x)
   123	
   124	    cases = {c: t for c, t in [(True, true_task), (False, false_task)] if t is not None}
   125	    if cases:
   126	        bool_condition = as_bool(condition, mapped=mapped)
   127	        switch(condition=bool_condition, cases=cases, mapped=mapped)
   128	
   129	
   130	def merge(*tasks: Task, flow=None, mapped: bool = False) -> Task:
   131	    """
   132	    Merges conditional branches back together.
   133	
   134	    A conditional branch in a flow results in one or more tasks proceeding and one or
   135	    more tasks skipping. It is often convenient to merge those branches back into a
   136	    single result. This function is a simple way to achieve that goal. By default this
   137	    task will skip if all its upstream dependencies are also skipped.
   138	
   139	    The merge will return the first real result it encounters, or `None`. If multiple
   140	    tasks might return a result, group them with a list.
   141	
   142	    Example:
   143	        ```python
   144	        with Flow("My Flow"):
   145	            true_branch = ActionIfTrue()
   146	            false_branch = ActionIfFalse()
   147	            ifelse(CheckCondition(), true_branch, false_branch)
   148	
   149	            merged_result = merge(true_branch, false_branch)
   150	        ```
   151	
   152	    Args:
   153	        - *tasks (Task): tasks whose results should be merged into a single result. The tasks are
   154	            assumed to all sit downstream of different `switch` branches, such that only
   155	            one of them will contain a result and the others will all be skipped.
   156	        - flow (Flow, optional): The flow to use, defaults to the current flow
   157	            in context if no flow is specified
   158	        - mapped (bool, optional): If true, the `merge` operation will be mapped over the
   159	            arguments instead of applied directly. Defaults to `False`.
   160	
   161	    Returns:
   162	        - Task: a Task representing the merged result.
   163	
   164	    """
   165	    return Merge().bind(
   166	        **{"task_{}".format(i + 1): t for i, t in enumerate(tasks)},
   167	        flow=flow,
   168	        mapped=mapped
   169	    )
   170	
</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Perfect! Now I understand the structure. """
Debug script to understand task types in the full failing case.
"""

from prefect import Flow, task, case, apply_map, Parameter, unmapped
from prefect.tasks.control_flow import merge
from prefect.tasks.control_flow.conditional import CompareValue, Merge

@task
def generate_input1():
    return{
        "num":1,
        "text": "hello"
    }

@task  
def generate_input2():
    return {
        "num":2,
        "text": "bye"
    }


@task
def inc(input1, squared):
    return (input1["num"] + 1)**squared

@task
def negate(input1, input2):
    # Fixed the method - extend doesn't work on dict, let's concatenate text
    return input1["text"] + input2["text"]

@task
def is_even(input1):
    return input1["num"] % 2 == 0

def inc_or_negate(input1, input2, squared):
    
    cond = is_even(input1)

    # If x is even, increment it
    with case(cond, True):
        res1 = inc(input1, unmapped(squared))

    # If x is odd, negate it
    with case(cond, False):
        res2 = negate(input1, input2)
    
    return merge(res1, res2)

def main():
    print("Creating flow...")
    with Flow("debug") as flow:
        squared = Parameter("squared", default=2)
        input1 = generate_input1.map()
        input2 = generate_input2.map()
        result = apply_map(inc_or_negate, input1, input2, squared)

    print("Task types:")
    for task in flow.tasks:
        print(f"  {task.name}: {type(task)}")
        if isinstance(task, CompareValue):
            print(f"    -> This is a CompareValue task")
        elif isinstance(task, Merge):
            print(f"    -> This is a Merge task")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>File created successfully at: /workspace/PrefectHQ__prefect__0.14/debug_types_full.py</tool_response><|assistant|></think><tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>cd /workspace/PrefectHQ__prefect__0.14 && python debug_types_full.py</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Creating flow...

## Phase 6. FIX IMPLEMENTATION

The solution is to exclude control flow tasks (`CompareValue` and `Merge`) from getting the blanket upstream dependencies in the `apply_map` function. These tasks have special semantics and should only depend on their intended upstream tasks.<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/PrefectHQ__prefect__0.14/src/prefect/utilities/tasks.py</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>    # Any task created inside `apply_map` must have a transitive dependency to
    # all of the inputs to apply_map, except for unmapped constants.  This
    # ensures three things:
    #
    # - All mapped arguments must have the same length. supporting disparate
    # lengths leads to odd semantics.
    #
    # - Tasks created by `apply_map` conceptually share an upstream dependency
    # tree. This matches the causality you'd expect if you were running as
    # normal eager python code - the stuff inside the `apply_map` only runs if
    # the inputs are completed, not just the inputs that certain subcomponents
    # depend on.
    #
    # - Tasks with no external dependencies are treated the same as tasks with
    # external deps (we need to add upstream_tasks to tasks created in `func`
    # with no external deps to get them to run as proper map tasks).  We add
    # upstream tasks uniformly for all tasks, not just ones without external
    # deps - the uniform behavior makes this easier to reason about.
    #
    # Here we do a final pass adding missing upstream deps on mapped arguments
    # to all newly created tasks in the apply_map.
    new_tasks = flow2.tasks.difference(arg_info)
    for task in new_tasks:
        upstream_tasks = flow.upstream_tasks(task)
        is_root_in_subgraph = not upstream_tasks.intersection(new_tasks)
        if is_root_in_subgraph:
            for arg_task, (is_mapped, is_constant) in arg_info.items():
                # Add all args except unmapped constants as direct
                # upstream tasks if they're not already upstream tasks
                if arg_task not in upstream_tasks and (is_mapped or not is_constant):
                    flow.add_edge(
                        upstream_task=arg_task, downstream_task=task, mapped=is_mapped
                    )</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>    # Any task created inside `apply_map` must have a transitive dependency to

    # lengths leads to odd semantics.
    #
    # - Tasks created by `apply_map` conceptually share an upstream dependency
    # tree. This matches the causality you'd expect if you were running as
    # normal eager python code - the stuff inside the `apply_map` only runs if
    # the inputs are completed, not just the inputs that certain subcomponents
    # depend on.
    #
    # - Tasks with no external dependencies are treated the same as tasks with
    # external deps (we need to add upstream_tasks to tasks created in `func`
    # with no external deps to get them to run as proper map tasks).  We add
    # upstream tasks uniformly for all tasks, not just ones without external
    # deps - the uniform behavior makes this easier to reason about.
    #
    # However, we should avoid adding dependencies that would create cycles or
    # that conflict with the special semantics of control flow tasks.
    #

    # to all newly created tasks in the apply_map.

    from prefect.tasks.control_flow.conditional import CompareValue, Merge

Let me add some debugging to see what's happening:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/PrefectHQ__prefect__0.14/debug_apply_map.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3

"""
Debug script to understand what's happening in apply_map.
"""

# Monkey patch the apply_map function to add debugging
import prefect.utilities.tasks
original_apply_map = prefect.utilities.tasks.apply_map

def debug_apply_map(func, *args, flow=None, **kwargs):
    print("DEBUG: Starting apply_map")
    result = original_apply_map(func, *args, flow=flow, **kwargs)
    print("DEBUG: Finished apply_map")
    return result

prefect.utilities.tasks.apply_map = debug_apply_map

from prefect import Flow, task, case, apply_map, Parameter, unmapped
from prefect.tasks.control_flow import merge
from prefect.tasks.control_flow.conditional import CompareValue, Merge

@task
def generate_input1():
    return{
        "num":1,
        "text": "hello"
    }

@task  
def generate_input2():
    return {
        "num":2,
        "text": "bye"
    }


@task
def inc(input1, squared):
    return (input1["num"] + 1)**squared

@task
def negate(input1, input2):
    # Fixed the method - extend doesn't work on dict, let's concatenate text
    return input1["text"] + input2["text"]

@task
def is_even(input1):
    return input1["num"] % 2 == 0

def inc_or_negate(input1, input2, squared):
    
    cond = is_even(input1)

    # If x is even, increment it
    with case(cond, True):
        res1 = inc(input1, unmapped(squared))

    # If x is odd, negate it
    with case(cond, False):
        res2 = negate(input1, input2)
    
    return merge(res1, res2)

def main():
    print("Creating flow...")
    with Flow("debug") as flow:
        squared = Parameter("squared", default=2)
        input1 = generate_input1.map()
        input2 = generate_input2.map()
        result = apply_map(inc_or_negate, input1, input2, squared)

    print(f"Total tasks: {len(flow.tasks)}")
    print(f"Total edges: {len(flow.edges)}")

if __name__ == "__main__":

Let me revert and try a simpler approach:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/PrefectHQ__prefect__0.14/src/prefect/utilities/tasks.py</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>    # Any task created inside `apply_map` must have a transitive dependency to
    # all of the inputs to apply_map, except for unmapped constants.  This
    # ensures three things:
    #
    # - All mapped arguments must have the same length. supporting disparate
    # lengths leads to odd semantics.
    #
    # - Tasks created by `apply_map` conceptually share an upstream dependency
    # tree. This matches the causality you'd expect if you were running as
    # normal eager python code - the stuff inside the `apply_map` only runs if
    # the inputs are completed, not just the inputs that certain subcomponents
    # depend on.
    #
    # - Tasks with no external dependencies are treated the same as tasks with
    # external deps (we need to add upstream_tasks to tasks created in `func`
    # with no external deps to get them to run as proper map tasks).  We add
    # upstream tasks uniformly for all tasks, not just ones without external
    # deps - the uniform behavior makes this easier to reason about.
    #
    # However, we should avoid adding dependencies that would create cycles or
    # that conflict with the special semantics of control flow tasks.
    #
    # Here we do a final pass adding missing upstream deps on mapped arguments
    # to all newly created tasks in the apply_map.
    new_tasks = flow2.tasks.difference(arg_info)
    for task in new_tasks:
        # Skip control flow tasks which have special dependency semantics
        # Check by name pattern since we can't easily import the classes here
        if task.name.startswith('case(') or task.name == 'Merge':
            continue
            
        upstream_tasks = flow.upstream_tasks(task)
        is_root_in_subgraph = not upstream_tasks.intersection(new_tasks)
        if is_root_in_subgraph:
            for arg_task, (is_mapped, is_constant) in arg_info.items():
                # Add all args except unmapped constants as direct
                # upstream tasks if they're not already upstream tasks
                if arg_task not in upstream_tasks and (is_mapped or not is_constant):
                    flow.add_edge(
                        upstream_task=arg_task, downstream_task=task, mapped=is_mapped

Let me add some debugging to see what edges exist in `flow2`:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/PrefectHQ__prefect__0.14/debug_flow2.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3

"""
Debug script to understand what's in flow2.
"""

# Monkey patch the apply_map function to add debugging
import prefect.utilities.tasks

# Save the original function
original_apply_map = prefect.utilities.tasks.apply_map

def debug_apply_map(func, *args, flow=None, **kwargs):
    print("DEBUG: Starting apply_map")
    
    # Get access to the internal logic
    import prefect
    import itertools
    from prefect.utilities import edges
    
    no_flow_provided = flow is None
    if no_flow_provided:
        flow = prefect.context.get("flow", None)
        if flow is None:
            raise ValueError("Couldn't infer a flow in the current context")
    assert isinstance(flow, prefect.Flow)  # appease mypy

    # Check if args/kwargs are valid first
    for x in itertools.chain(args, kwargs.values()):
        if not isinstance(
            x, (prefect.Task, prefect.utilities.edges.EdgeAnnotation, list, tuple, range)
        ):
            raise TypeError(
                f"Cannot map over non-sequence object of type `{type(x).__name__}`"
            )

    flow2 = prefect.Flow("temporary flow")
    # A mapping of all the input args -> (is_mapped, is_constant)
    arg_info = {}
    # A mapping of the ids of all constants -> the Constant task.
    # Used to convert constants to constant tasks if needed
    id_to_const = {}

    # Preprocess inputs to `apply_map`:
    # - Extract information about each argument (is unmapped, is constant, ...)
    # - Convert all arguments to instances of `Task`
    # - Add all non-constant arguments to the flow. Constant arguments are
    #   added later as needed.
    def preprocess(a):
        from prefect.utilities.tasks import as_task
        a2 = as_task(a, flow=flow2)
        is_mapped = not isinstance(a, prefect.utilities.edges.unmapped)
        from prefect.tasks.core.constants import Constant
        is_constant = isinstance(a2, Constant)
        arg_info[a2] = (is_mapped, is_constant)
        if not is_constant:
            flow.add_task(a2)  # type: ignore
        if is_mapped and is_constant:
            id_to_const[id(a2.value)] = a2  # type: ignore
        return a2

    args2 = [preprocess(a) for a in args]
    kwargs2 = {k: preprocess(v) for k, v in kwargs.items()}
    
    print("DEBUG: About to construct temporary flow")
    print(f"DEBUG: flow2 initially has {len(flow2.tasks)} tasks and {len(flow2.edges)} edges")

    # Construct a temporary flow for the subgraph
    # We set case=None & resource=None to ignore any external case/resource
    # blocks while constructing the temporary flow.
    with prefect.context(mapped=True, case=None, resource=None):
        with flow2:
            if no_flow_provided:
                res = func(*args2, **kwargs2)
            else:
                res = func(*args2, flow=flow2, **kwargs2)
                
    print(f"DEBUG: After constructing temporary flow, flow2 has {len(flow2.tasks)} tasks and {len(flow2.edges)} edges")
    print("DEBUG: Tasks in flow2:")
    for i, task in enumerate(flow2.tasks):
        print(f"  {i}: {task.name}")
        
    print("DEBUG: Edges in flow2:")
    for i, edge in enumerate(flow2.edges):
        print(f"  {i}: {edge.upstream_task.name} -> {edge.downstream_task.name} (mapped={edge.mapped})")
    
    # Call the original function to complete the process
    result = original_apply_map(func, *args, flow=flow, **kwargs)
    print("DEBUG: Finished apply_map")
    return result

prefect.utilities.tasks.apply_map = debug_apply_map

from prefect import Flow, task, case, apply_map, Parameter, unmapped
from prefect.tasks.control_flow import merge

@task
def generate_input1():
    return{
        "num":1,
        "text": "hello"
    }

@task  
def generate_input2():
    return {
        "num":2,
        "text": "bye"
    }


@task
def inc(input1, squared):
    return (input1["num"] + 1)**squared

@task
def negate(input1, input2):
    # Fixed the method - extend doesn't work on dict, let's concatenate text
    return input1["text"] + input2["text"]

@task
def is_even(input1):
    return input1["num"] % 2 == 0

def inc_or_negate(input1, input2, squared):
    
    cond = is_even(input1)

    # If x is even, increment it
    with case(cond, True):
        res1 = inc(input1, unmapped(squared))

    # If x is odd, negate it
    with case(cond, False):
        res2 = negate(input1, input2)
    
    return merge(res1, res2)

def main():
    print("Creating flow...")
    with Flow("debug") as flow:
        squared = Parameter("squared", default=2)
        input1 = generate_input1.map()
        input2 = generate_input2.map()
        result = apply_map(inc_or_negate, input1, input2, squared)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>File created successfully at: /workspace/PrefectHQ__prefect__0.14/debug_flow2.py</tool_response><|assistant|></think><tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>cd /workspace/PrefectHQ__prefect__0.14 && python debug_flow2.py</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Creating flow...
[The command completed with exit code 0.]
[Current working directory: /workspace/PrefectHQ__prefect__0.14]
[Python interpreter: /opt/conda/envs/testbed/bin/python]
[Command finished with exit code 0]</tool_response><|assistant|></think>That's strange, no output. Let me fix the debug script:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/PrefectHQ__prefect__0.14/debug_flow2_fixed.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3

"""
Debug script to understand what's in flow2.
"""

from prefect import Flow, task, case, apply_map, Parameter, unmapped
from prefect.tasks.control_flow import merge

@task
def generate_input1():
    return{
        "num":1,
        "text": "hello"
    }

@task  
def generate_input2():
    return {
        "num":2,
        "text": "bye"
    }


@task
def inc(input1, squared):
    return (input1["num"] + 1)**squared

@task
def negate(input1, input2):
    # Fixed the method - extend doesn't work on dict, let's concatenate text
    return input1["text"] + input2["text"]

@task
def is_even(input1):
    return input1["num"] % 2 == 0

def inc_or_negate(input1, input2, squared):
    
    cond = is_even(input1)

    # If x is even, increment it
    with case(cond, True):
        res1 = inc(input1, unmapped(squared))

    # If x is odd, negate it
    with case(cond, False):
        res2 = negate(input1, input2)
    
    return merge(res1, res2)

# Let me monkey patch inside the apply_map function directly
import prefect.utilities.tasks
original_apply_map_inner = prefect.utilities.tasks.apply_map

def debug_apply_map_inner(func, *args, flow=None, **kwargs):
    print("DEBUG: Starting apply_map")
    
    import prefect
    import itertools
    from prefect.utilities import edges
    from prefect.tasks.core.constants import Constant
    
    no_flow_provided = flow is None
    if no_flow_provided:
        flow = prefect.context.get("flow", None)
        if flow is None:
            raise ValueError("Couldn't infer a flow in the current context")
    assert isinstance(flow, prefect.Flow)  # appease mypy

    # Check if args/kwargs are valid first
    for x in itertools.chain(args, kwargs.values()):
        if not isinstance(
            x, (prefect.Task, prefect.utilities.edges.EdgeAnnotation, list, tuple, range)
        ):
            raise TypeError(
                f"Cannot map over non-sequence object of type `{type(x).__name__}`"
            )

    flow2 = prefect.Flow("temporary flow")
    # A mapping of all the input args -> (is_mapped, is_constant)
    arg_info = {}
    # A mapping of the ids of all constants -> the Constant task.
    # Used to convert constants to constant tasks if needed
    id_to_const = {}

    # Preprocess inputs to `apply_map`:
    # - Extract information about each argument (is unmapped, is constant, ...)
    # - Convert all arguments to instances of `Task`
    # - Add all non-constant arguments to the flow. Constant arguments are
    #   added later as needed.
    def preprocess(a):
        from prefect.utilities.tasks import as_task
        a2 = as_task(a, flow=flow2)
        is_mapped = not isinstance(a, prefect.utilities.edges.unmapped)
        is_constant = isinstance(a2, Constant)
        arg_info[a2] = (is_mapped, is_constant)
        if not is_constant:
            flow.add_task(a2)  # type: ignore
        if is_mapped and is_constant:
            id_to_const[id(a2.value)] = a2  # type: ignore
        return a2

    args2 = [preprocess(a) for a in args]
    kwargs2 = {k: preprocess(v) for k, v in kwargs.items()}
    
    print("DEBUG: About to construct temporary flow")
    print(f"DEBUG: flow2 initially has {len(flow2.tasks)} tasks and {len(flow2.edges)} edges")

    # Construct a temporary flow for the subgraph
    # We set case=None & resource=None to ignore any external case/resource
    # blocks while constructing the temporary flow.
    with prefect.context(mapped=True, case=None, resource=None):
        with flow2:
            if no_flow_provided:
                res = func(*args2, **kwargs2)
            else:
                res = func(*args2, flow=flow2, **kwargs2)
                
    print(f"DEBUG: After constructing temporary flow, flow2 has {len(flow2.tasks)} tasks and {len(flow2.edges)} edges")
    print("DEBUG: Tasks in flow2:")
    for i, task in enumerate(flow2.tasks):
        print(f"  {i}: {task.name} ({type(task)})")
        
    print("DEBUG: Edges in flow2:")
    for i, edge in enumerate(flow2.edges):
        print(f"  {i}: {edge.upstream_task.name} -> {edge.downstream_task.name} (mapped={edge.mapped})")
    
    # Now call the original function but let's see what it does
    # Actually, let's just reimplement the rest to avoid double-execution
    print("DEBUG: Continuing with original logic...")
    
    # Copy over all tasks in the subgraph
    for task in flow2.tasks:
        flow.add_task(task)

    # Copy over all edges, updating any non-explicitly-unmapped edges to mapped
    print("DEBUG: Copying edges from flow2...")
    for edge in flow2.edges:
        print(f"DEBUG: Copying edge: {edge.upstream_task.name} -> {edge.downstream_task.name}")
        flow.add_edge(
            upstream_task=edge.upstream_task,
            downstream_task=edge.downstream_task,
            key=edge.key,
            mapped=arg_info.get(edge.upstream_task, (True,))[0],
        )

    # Copy over all constants, updating any constants that should be mapped
    # to be tasks rather than stored in the constants mapping
    for task, constants in flow2.constants.items():
        for key, c in constants.items():
            if id(c) in id_to_const:
                c_task = id_to_const[id(c)]
                flow.add_task(c_task)
                flow.add_edge(
                    upstream_task=c_task, downstream_task=task, key=key, mapped=True
                )
            else:
                flow.constants[task][key] = c
                
    print("DEBUG: Done copying")

    # The rest of the original logic would go here, but let's just return
    return res

# Apply the monkey patch
prefect.utilities.tasks.apply_map = debug_apply_map_inner

def main():
    print("Creating flow...")
    with Flow("debug") as flow:
        squared = Parameter("squared", default=2)
        input1 = generate_input1.map()
        input2 = generate_input2.map()
        result = apply_map(inc_or_negate, input1, input2, squared)

`10: case(False) -> generate_input2 (mapped=False)`

This edge is being created during the construction of the temporary flow, before my fix even gets a chance to run. The edge is created in the `case` logic itself.

Looking at the edges in `flow2`:
- Edge 10: `case(False) -> generate_input2 (mapped=False)` - This is the problematic one!

This means the issue is in how the `case` context manager works. When `case(cond, False)` is executed, it's somehow creating a dependency on `generate_input2`.

Let me look more carefully at the case logic. In `case.py`, the `__exit__` method (lines 91-100) creates a `CompareValue` task and sets dependencies. The key line is 99-100:

```python
if not self._tasks.intersection(self._flow.upstream_tasks(child)):
    child.set_upstream(cond, flow=self._flow)
```

This says: if a task created in the case block has no upstream tasks from within the same case block, then set the case condition as an upstream task.

But there's something wrong here. Let me check what tasks are in `self._tasks` for the `case(False)` block.

Looking at the function:
```python
def inc_or_negate(input1, input2, squared):
    cond = is_even(input1)
    with case(cond, True):
        res1 = inc(input1, unmapped(squared))
    with case(cond, False):
        res2 = negate(input1, input2)
    return merge(res1, res2)
```

In the `case(False)` block, only `negate` is created. But somehow `generate_input2` is being considered as part of the case block's tasks.

Wait, let me re-read the case logic more carefully. The issue might be that `generate_input2` is being added to `self._tasks` somehow.

Looking at `case.py` line 66-79:
```python
def add_task(self, task: Task, flow: Flow) -> None:
    """Add a new task under the case statement.
    Args:
        - task (Task): the task to add
        - flow (Flow): the flow to use
    """
    if self._flow is None:
        self._flow = flow
    elif self._flow is not flow:
        raise ValueError(
            "Multiple flows cannot be used with the same case statement"
        )
    self._tasks.add(task)
```

And in `case.py` line 81, in `__enter__`, it sets `prefect.context.update(case=self)`. Then when tasks are created, they check if there's a case in context and call `add_task`.

So the issue is that `generate_input2` is being created while the `case(False)` context is active, and therefore it's being added to `self._tasks`.

But `generate_input2` shouldn't be created in the `case(False)` block at all! Let me trace this:

In `inc_or_negate`:
1. `cond = is_even(input1)` - outside any case
2. `with case(cond, True):` - enter case(True) context
3. `res1 = inc(input1, unmapped(squared))` - inc gets added to case(True) 
4. exit case(True) context
5. `with case(cond, False):` - enter case(False) context  
6. `res2 = negate(input1, input2)` - negate gets added to case(False)
7. exit case(False) context

But `input1` and `input2` are parameters passed into the function. They shouldn't be created inside the case blocks.

Wait, I think I see the issue. The problem is that `input1` and `input2` are the mapped arguments. When `apply_map` is used, these are the tasks that represent the individual elements being mapped over.

But in the temporary flow construction, somehow `generate_input2` is being treated as if it's created within the case block.

Let me think about this differently. Maybe the issue is in how `apply_map` works with the mapped context.

Looking at the debug output, I notice that in `flow2`, ALL the edges have `mapped=False`. This is because they're created in the temporary flow without the mapped context properly set up for the argument tasks.

But then when these edges are copied to the main flow, they get `mapped=True` for the mapped arguments.

The real issue is edge 10 in flow2: `case(False) -> generate_input2 (mapped=False)`. This edge shouldn't exist.

Let me check if there are tasks being created in the wrong context. The issue might be that when `negate(input1, input2)` is called, somehow `input2` is being registered with the case.

Actually, let me check what exactly is happening. In the case logic, the dependency setting happens in lines 96-100:

```python
for child in self._tasks:
    # If a task has no upstream tasks created in this case block,
    # the case conditional should be set as an upstream task.
    if not self._tasks.intersection(self._flow.upstream_tasks(child)):
        child.set_upstream(cond, flow=self._flow)
```

So for each task in the case block, if it doesn't have upstream tasks that are also in the case block, then the case condition becomes its upstream.

For `negate`, its upstream tasks are `input1` and `input2`. Neither of these are in `self._tasks` (since they were created outside the case block), so `negate` gets the case condition as upstream.

But then why does `case(False)` get `generate_input2` as downstream?

Wait, I think I misunderstood the direction. The edge `case(False) -> generate_input2` means `case(False)` is upstream of `generate_input2`, not downstream.

So `generate_input2` has `case(False)` as an upstream task. But `generate_input2` is an argument to the `apply_map`, so it should not be created inside the case block at all.

Let me look at this more carefully. The edge in flow2 is:
`10: case(False) -> generate_input2 (mapped=False)`

This means `case(False)` is upstream of `generate_input2`. But `generate_input2` is a mapped argument task, it should be created during the preprocessing phase, outside of any case context.

Let me check the order of operations in `apply_map`:
1. Preprocessing - convert args to tasks, add non-constants to main flow
2. Create temporary flow
3. Execute `func` in temporary flow context
4. Copy tasks and edges from temporary flow to main flow
5. Add blanket dependencies

The issue must be in step 3 - during the execution of `func` in the temporary flow context.

But `generate_input2` should already exist as a task in `args2`. Let me check...

Actually, let me step back and think about the real issue. The problem is not that `generate_input2` depends on `case(False)`. The problem is that `case(False)` depends on `generate_input2`, creating the cycle:

`is_even` -> `case(False)` -> `generate_input2` -> `is_even`

Because:
- `is_even` depends on `generate_input1` and `generate_input2` (correct)
- `case(False)` depends on `is_even` (correct) 
- `case(False)` depends on `generate_input2` (incorrect!)
- `generate_input2` depends on `is_even` (this edge might not exist, let me check...)

Looking at the debug output again:
Upstream -> Downstream relationships:
  generate_input2: ['negate', 'is_even']  // generate_input2 -> is_even EXISTS
  case(False): ['generate_input2']        // case(False) -> generate_input2 EXISTS

So we have:
1. `is_even` -> `case(False)` (from case logic)
2. `case(False)` -> `generate_input2` (the problematic edge)
3. `generate_input2` -> `is_even` (this shouldn't exist!)

Wait, edge 3 shouldn't exist based on the function logic. `is_even` takes `input1` as argument, not `input2`.

Let me look at the original function:
```python
@task
def is_even(input1):
    return input1["num"] % 2 == 0
```

So `is_even` should only depend on `input1`, not `input2`. But in the debug output I see:
`generate_input2 -> is_even (mapped=True)`

`4: generate_input1 -> is_even (mapped=False)` - This is correct  
`10: case(False) -> generate_input2 (mapped=False)` - This is wrong
